f['general']['optophysiology']['ImagingPlane']['imaging_rate'][()]\n   316→\n   317→        # ---- Load behavioral data ----\n   318→        behav = f['processing']['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries']\n   319→        position = behav['position']['data'][:]\n   320→        speed = behav['speed']['data'][:]\n   321→        lick = behav['lick']['data'][:]\n   322→        reward_zone_signal = behav['reward_zone']['data'][:]\n   323→        trial_start_signal = behav['trial_start']['data'][:]\n   324→        teleport_signal = behav['teleport']['data'][:]\n   325→        trial_number = behav['trial number']['data'][:]\n   326→        environment = behav['environment']['data'][:]\n   327→        scanning = behav['scanning']['data'][:]\n   328→\n   329→        # Reward events\n   330→        reward_data = behav['Reward']['data'][:]\n   331→        reward_timestamps = behav['Reward']['timestamps'][:]\n   332→        behav_timestamps = behav['position']['timestamps'][:]\n   333→\n   334→        # ---- Load neural data ----\n   335→        ophys = f['processing']['ophys']\n   336→        seg = ophys['ImageSegmentation']['PlaneSegmentation']\n   337→        iscell = seg['iscell'][:, 0].astype(bool)\n   338→        plane_idx = seg['planeIdx'][:]\n   339→\n   340→        # Load fluorescence and deconvolved for all planes\n   341→        planes = sorted(ophys['Deconvolved'].keys())\n   342→\n   343→        if len(planes) == 1:\n   344→            deconv_data = ophys['Deconvolved']['plane0']['data'][:]  # (n_samples, n_rois)\n   345→            fluor_data = ophys['Fluorescence']['plane0']['data'][:]\n   346→            neuropil_data = ophys['Neuropil']['plane0']['data'][:]\n   347→            n_planes = 1\n   348→        else:\n   349→            # Multi-plane: concatenate ROIs across planes\n   350→            deconv_parts = []\n   351→            fluor_parts = []\n   352→            neuro_parts = []\n   353→            for plane in planes:\n   354→                deconv_parts.append(ophys['Deconvolved'][plane]['data'][:])\n   355→                fluor_parts.append(ophys['Fluorescence'][plane]['data'][:])\n   356→                neuro_parts.append(ophys['Neuropil'][plane]['data'][:])\n   357→            deconv_data = np.concatenate(deconv_parts, axis=1)\n   358→            fluor_data = np.concatenate(fluor_parts, axis=1)\n   359→            neuropil_data = np.concatenate(neuro_parts, axis=1)\n   360→            n_planes = len(planes)\n   361→\n   362→    t_load = time.time() - t0\n   363→\n   364→    # Handle potential length mismatch between behavioral and neural data\n   365→    n_behav_samples = len(position)\n   366→    n_neural_samples = deconv_data.shape[0]\n   367→    n_samples = min(n_behav_samples, n_neural_samples)\n   368→    if n_behav_samples != n_neural_samples:\n   369→        # Truncate to common length\n   370→        position = position[:n_samples]\n   371→        speed = speed[:n_samples]\n   372→        lick = lick[:n_samples]\n   373→        reward_zone_signal = reward_zone_signal[:n_samples]\n   374→        trial_start_signal = trial_start_signal[:n_samples]\n   375→        teleport_signal = teleport_signal[:n_samples]\n   376→        trial_number = trial_number[:n_samples]\n   377→        environment = environment[:n_samples]\n   378→        scanning = scanning[:n_samples]\n   379→        behav_timestamps = behav_timestamps[:n_samples]\n   380→        deconv_data = deconv_data[:n_samples, :]\n   381→        fluor_data = fluor_data[:n_samples, :]\n   382→        neuropil_data = neuropil_data[:n_samples, :]\n   383→\n   384→    n_total_rois = deconv_data.shape[1]\n   385→\n   386→    # ---- Find trial boundaries ----\n   387→    trial_starts = np.where(trial_start_signal > 0)[0]\n   388→    teleports = np.where(teleport_signal > 0)[0]\n   389→\n   390→    # Ensure matching number of starts and teleports\n   391→    n_trials = min(len(trial_starts), len(teleports))\n   392→    trial_starts = trial_starts[:n_trials]\n   393→    teleports = teleports[:n_trials]\n   394→\n   395→    # Ensure each teleport comes after its corresponding trial start\n   396→    valid = teleports > trial_starts\n   397→    trial_starts = trial_starts[valid]\n   398→    teleports = teleports[valid]\n   399→    n_trials = len(trial_starts)\n   400→\n   401→    # ---- Compute dF/F for interneuron detection ----\n   402→    t1 = time.time()\n   403→    dff = compute_dff(fluor_data, neuropil_data, trial_starts, teleports)\n   404→    t_dff = time.time() - t1\n   405→\n   406→    # ---- Identify interneurons ----\n   407→    t1 = time.time()\n   408→    is_interneuron = identify_interneurons(dff, speed, iscell)\n   409→    t_int = time.time() - t1\n   410→\n   411→    # Create final neuron mask: iscell AND not interneuron\n   412→    iscell_indices = np.where(iscell)[0]\n   413→    non_interneuron = ~is_interneuron\n   414→    final_neuron_mask = np.zeros(n_total_rois, dtype=bool)\n   415→    final_neuron_mask[iscell_indices[non_interneuron]] = True\n   416→\n   417→    n_neurons = final_neuron_mask.sum()\n   418→    n_interneurons = is_interneuron.sum()\n   419→\n   420→    # ---- Get deconvolved data for selected neurons ----\n   421→    neural_all = deconv_data[:, final_neuron_mask]  # (n_samples, n_neurons)\n   422→\n   423→    # ---- Process reward events ----\n   424→    # Map reward timestamps to frame indices\n   425→    reward_frame_indices = np.searchsorted(behav_timestamps, reward_timestamps)\n   426→    reward_frame_indices = np.clip(reward_frame_indices, 0, n_samples - 1)\n   427→\n   428→    # Determine which trials have rewards\n   429→    trial_rewarded = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=bool)\n   430→    for t in range(n_trials):\n   431→        start = trial_starts[t]\n   432→        end = teleports[t]\n   433→        trial_rewards = np.any((reward_frame_indices >= start) & (reward_frame_indices < end))\n   434→        trial_rewarded[t] = trial_rewards\n   435→\n   436→    # ---- Identify reward zones per trial ----\n   437→    trial_rz_label = []  # 'A', 'B', 'C', or None\n   438→    trial_rz_start = np.zeros(n_trials)\n   439→    trial_rz_end = np.zeros(n_trials)\n   440→\n   441→    # First pass: identify zones where animal entered\n   442→    last_known_zone = None\n   443→    for t in range(n_trials):\n   444→        zone = identify_reward_zone(position, reward_zone_signal, trial_starts[t], teleports[t])\n   445→        if zone is not None:\n   446→            last_known_zone = zone\n   447→        trial_rz_label.append(zone if zone is not None else last_known_zone)\n   448→\n   449→    # Fill backward for any leading None values\n   450→    if trial_rz_label[0] is None:\n   451→        for t in range(n_trials):\n   452→            if trial_rz_label[t] is not None:\n   453→                for tt in range(t):\n   454→                    trial_rz_label[tt] = trial_rz_label[t]\n   455→                break\n   456→\n   457→    # Set reward zone coordinates\n   458→    for t in range(n_trials):\n   459→        if trial_rz_label[t] is not None:\n   460→            trial_rz_start[t], trial_rz_end[t] = REWARD_ZONES[trial_rz_label[t]]\n   461→        else:\n   462→            # Fallback - shouldn't happen\n   463→            trial_rz_start[t], trial_rz_end[t] = 200, 250\n   464→\n   465→    # Map reward zone labels to indices: A=0, B=1, C=2\n   466→    rz_label_to_idx = {'A': 0, 'B': 1, 'C': 2}\n   467→    trial_rz_idx = np.array([rz_label_to_idx.get(lbl, 0) for lbl in trial_rz_label])\n   468→\n   469→    # ---- Process lick data ----\n   470→    # Following reference: binarize licks (clip to 0/1), apply error correction\n   471→    lick_binary = np.clip(lick, 0, 1).astype(np.float64)\n   472→\n   473→    # Lick error correction per trial\n   474→    lick_error_trials = []\n   475→    for t in range(n_trials):\n   476→        start = trial_starts[t]\n   477→        end = teleports[t]\n   478→        trial_lick = lick[start:end]\n   479→        if len(trial_lick) > 0:\n   480→            frac_bad = np.sum(trial_lick > 2) / len(trial_lick)\n   481→            if frac_bad > LICK_ERROR_FRACTION:\n   482→                lick_binary[start:end] = np.nan\n   483→                lick_error_trials.append(t)\n   484→\n   485→    # ---- Get environment per trial ----\n   486→    trial_env = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=np.int64)\n   487→    for t in range(n_trials):\n   488→        start = trial_starts[t]\n   489→        end = teleports[t]\n   490→        env_vals = environment[start:end]\n   491→        valid_env = env_vals[env_vals >= 0]\n   492→        if len(valid_env) > 0:\n   493→            trial_env[t] = int(np.median(valid_env))\n   494→        else:\n   495→            trial_env[t] = 0\n   496→\n   497→    # ---- Previous trial outcome ----\n   498→    prev_trial_outcome = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=np.int64)\n   499→    for t in range(1, n_trials):\n   500→        prev_trial_outcome[t] = int(trial_rewarded[t - 1])\n   501→    # First trial: no previous, default to 0 (unknown/omitted)\n   502→\n   503→    # ---- Build per-trial data ----\n   504→    neural_trials = []\n   505→    input_trials = []\n   506→    output_trials = []\n   507→    valid_trials = []\n   508→\n   509→    for t in range(n_trials):\n   510→        start = trial_starts[t]\n   511→        end = teleports[t]\n   512→        n_timepoints = end - start\n   513→\n   514→        if n_timepoints < 5:\n   515→            continue  # Skip very short trials\n   516→\n   517→        # Neural: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n   518→        trial_neural = neural_all[start:end, :].T.copy()  # (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n   519→\n   520→        # Replace NaN with 0 in neural data (following reference: X[np.isnan(X)] = 0)\n   521→        trial_neural[np.isnan(trial_neural)] = 0\n   522→\n   523→        # Input construction\n   524→        # [0] Time from trial start in seconds (time-varying)\n   525→        time_from_start = np.arange(n_timepoints) * FRAME_PERIOD\n   526→\n   527→        # [1] Environment type (per trial, broadcast)\n   528→        env_val = float(trial_env[t])\n   529→\n   530→        # [2] Trial number (per trial)\n   531→        trial_num = float(t)\n   532→\n   533→        # [3] Previous trial outcome (per trial)\n   534→        prev_outcome = float(prev_trial_outcome[t])\n   535→\n   536→        # Input array: (4,) for per-trial values, (4, n_timepoints) for time-varying\n   537→        # Time from start is time-varying; others are per-trial\n   538→        # Following the spec: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input,)\n   539→        # Since time_from_start is time-varying, we need mixed format\n   540→        # Use (4,) for per-trial inputs and time_from_start as time-varying\n   541→        # Actually, the spec says input can be (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n   542→        # Let's make time_from_start time-varying and others per-trial\n   543→        # We'll pack as: row 0 = time_from_start (time-varying)\n   544→        # rows 1-3 = per-trial values broadcast to time-varying\n   545→        trial_input = np.zeros((4, n_timepoints), dtype=np.float32)\n   546→        trial_input[0, :] = time_from_start\n   547→        trial_input[1, :] = env_val\n   548→        trial_input[2, :] = trial_num\n   549→        trial_input[3, :] = prev_outcome\n   550→\n   551→        # Output construction\n   552→        trial_pos = position[start:end]\n   553→        trial_speed = speed[start:end]\n   554→        trial_lick = lick_binary[start:end]\n   555→\n   556→        # [0] Distance to reward zone (time-varying, discretized)\n   557→        dist_to_rz = compute_distance_to_reward_zone(trial_pos, trial_rz_start[t], trial_rz_end[t])\n   558→        dist_bins = discretize_distance(dist_to_rz)\n   559→\n   560→        # [1] Absolute position (time-varying, discretized)\n   561→        pos_bins = discretize_position(trial_pos)\n   562→\n   563→        # [2] Speed (time-varying, discretized)\n   564→        speed_bins = discretize_speed(trial_speed)\n   565→\n   566→        # [3] Lick (time-varying, binary)\n   567→        lick_vals = np.where(np.isnan(trial_lick), 0, trial_lick).astype(np.int64)\n   568→\n   569→        # [4] Reward zone location (per trial)\n   570→        rz_loc = trial_rz_idx[t]\n   571→\n   572→        # [5] Reward outcome (per trial)\n   573→        reward_out = int(trial_rewarded[t])\n   574→\n   575→        # Output: time-varying outputs as (n_output, n_timepoints), per-trial as (n_output,)\n   576→        # Mix: first 4 are time-varying, last 2 are per-trial\n   577→        # Pack all as (6, n_timepoints) with per-trial broadcast\n   578→        trial_output = np.zeros((6, n_timepoints), dtype=np.int64)\n   579→        trial_output[0, :] = dist_bins\n   580→        trial_output[1, :] = pos_bins\n   581→        trial_output[2, :] = speed_bins\n   582→        trial_output[3, :] = lick_vals\n   583→        trial_output[4, :] = rz_loc  # broadcast per-trial\n   584→        trial_output[5, :] = reward_out  # broadcast per-trial\n   585→\n   586→        neural_trials.append(trial_neural.astype(np.float32))\n   587→        input_trials.append(trial_input)\n   588→        output_trials.append(trial_output)\n   589→        valid_trials.append(t)\n   590→\n   591→    t_total = time.time() - t0\n   592→\n   593→    session_info = {\n   594→        'subject': subject_id,\n   595→        'session': session_id,\n   596→        'n_neurons': n_neurons,\n   597→        'n_interneurons': n_interneurons,\n   598→        'n_iscell': int(iscell.sum()),\n   599→        'n_trials': len(valid_trials),\n   600→        'n_trials_total': n_trials,\n   601→        'n_lick_error_trials': len(lick_error_trials),\n   602→        'n_rewarded': int(trial_rewarded.sum()),\n   603→        'imaging_rate': imaging_rate,\n   604→        'n_planes': n_planes,\n   605→        'load_time': t_load,\n   606→        'dff_time': t_dff,\n   607→        'interneuron_time': t_int,\n   608→        'total_time': t_total,\n   609→        'reward_zones': trial_rz_label,\n   610→        'environments': trial_env.tolist(),\n   611→    }\n   612→\n   613→    print(f\"  {subject_id} ses-{session_id}: {n_neurons} neurons ({n_interneurons} interneurons excluded), \"\n   614→          f\"{len(valid_trials)} trials, {int(trial_rewarded.sum())}/{n_trials} rewarded, \"\n   615→          f\"{len(lick_error_trials)} lick-error trials, \"\n   616→          f\"{t_total:.1f}s\")\n   617→\n   618→    # ---- Optional processing visualization ----\n   619→    if show_processing and session_idx < 2:\n   620→        plot_processing(filepath, subject_id, session_id,\n   621→                        position, speed, lick_binary, trial_starts, teleports,\n   622→                        neural_all, trial_rz_start, trial_rz_end,\n   623→                        trial_rewarded, trial_env, valid_trials,\n   624→                        neural_trials, input_trials, output_trials,\n   625→                        session_idx)\n   626→\n   627→    return neural_trials, input_trials, output_trials, session_info\n   628→\n   629→\n   630→def plot_processing(filepath, subject_id, session_id,\n   631→                    position, speed, lick, trial_starts, teleports,\n   632→                    neural_all, trial_rz_start, trial_rz_end,\n   633→                    trial_rewarded, trial_env, valid_trials,\n   634→                    neural_trials, input_trials, output_trials,\n   635→                    session_idx):\n   636→    \"\"\"Plot processing steps for visual verification.\"\"\"\n   637→    import matplotlib\n   638→    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n   639→    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n   640→\n   641→    fig, axes = plt.subplots(6, 2, figsize=(20, 24))\n   642→    fig.suptitle(f'Processing: {subject_id} ses-{session_id}', fontsize=16)\n   643→\n   644→    # Pick a representative trial\n   645→    trial_idx = min(5, len(valid_trials) - 1)\n   646→    t = valid_trials[trial_idx]\n   647→    start = trial_starts[t]\n   648→    end = teleports[t]\n   649→    n_tp = end - start\n   650→    time_sec = np.arange(n_tp) * FRAME_PERIOD\n   651→\n   652→    # Panel 1: Raw position and speed for one trial\n   653→    ax = axes[0, 0]\n   654→    ax.plot(time_sec, position[start:end], label='Position (cm)')\n   655→    ax.axhline(y=trial_rz_start[t], color='r', linestyle='--', label=f'RZ start ({trial_rz_start[t]:.0f} cm)')\n   656→    ax.axhline(y=trial_rz_end[t], color='r', linestyle=':', label=f'RZ end ({trial_rz_end[t]:.0f} cm)')\n   657→    ax.set_ylabel('Position (cm)')\n   658→    ax.set_xlabel('Time (s)')\n   659→    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\n   660→    ax.set_title(f'Trial {t}: Position')\n   661→\n   662→    ax = axes[0, 1]\n   663→    ax.plot(time_sec, speed[start:end], label='Speed (cm/s)')\n   664→    ax.axhline(y=SPEED_THRESHOLD, color='r', linestyle='--', label=f'Threshold ({SPEED_THRESHOLD} cm/s)')\n   665→    ax.set_ylabel('Speed (cm/s)')\n   666→    ax.set_xlabel('Time (s)')\n   667→    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\n   668→    ax.set_title(f'Trial {t}: Speed')\n   669→\n   670→    # Panel 2: Neural data (first 10 neurons)\n   671→    ax = axes[1, 0]\n   672→    n_show = min(10, neural_trials[trial_idx].shape[0])\n   673→    for i in range(n_show):\n   674→        ax.plot(time_sec, neural_trials[trial_idx][i, :] + i * 0.5, linewidth=0.5)\n   675→    ax.set_ylabel('Neuron (offset)')\n   676→    ax.set_xlabel('Time (s)')\n   677→    ax.set_title(f'Trial {t}: Deconvolved neural (first {n_show})')\n   678→\n   679→    # Panel 3: Discretized outputs\n   680→    ax = axes[1, 1]\n   681→    ax.plot(time_sec, output_trials[trial_idx][0, :], label='Dist to RZ ax = axes[2, 0]\n   688→    ax.plot(time_sec, output_trials[trial_idx][1, :], label='Position bin')\n   689→    ax.set_ylabel('Bin')\n   690→    ax.set_xlabel('Time (s)')\n   691→    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\n   692→    ax.set_title(f'Trial {t}: Position (discretized)')\n   693→\n   694→    ax = axes[2, 1]\n   695→    ax.plot(time_sec, output_trials[trial_idx][2, :], label='Speed bin')\n   696→    ax.set_ylabel('Bin')\n   697→    ax.set_xlabel('Time (s)')\n   698→    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\n   699→    ax.set_title(f'Trial {t}: Speed (discretized)')\n   700→\n   701→    # Panel 4: Lick and reward\n   702→    ax = axes[3, 0]\n   703→    ax.plot(time_sec, output_trials[trial_idx][3, :], label='Lick')\n   704→    ax.set_ylabel('Lick (0/1)')\n   705→    ax.set_xlabel('Time (s)')\n   706→    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\n   707→    ax.set_title(f'Trial {t}: Lick')\n   708→\n   709→    # Panel 5: Continuous distance to RZ overlay with position\n   710→    ax = axes[3, 1]\n   711→    dist = compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position[start:end], trial_rz_start[t], trial_rz_end[t])\n   712→    ax.plot(time_sec, dist, label='Distance to RZ (cm)')\n   713→    ax.axhline(y=0, color='k', linestyle='-', linewidth=0.5)\n   714→    ax.axhline(y=-50, color='gray', linestyle='--', linewidth=0.5)\n   715→    ax.axhline(y=50, color='gray', linestyle='--', linewidth=0.5)\n   716→    ax.axhline(y=-10, color='gray', linestyle=':', linewidth=0.5)\n   717→    ax.axhline(y=10, color='gray', linestyle=':', linewidth=0.5)\n   718→    ax.set_ylabel('Distance (cm)')\n   719→    ax.set_xlabel('Time (s)')\n   720→    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\n   721→    ax.set_title(f'Trial {t}: Continuous distance to RZ')\n   722→\n   723→    # Panel 6: Session-level statistics\n   724→    ax = axes[4, 0]\n   725→    rz_counts = [sum(1 for lbl in [None]*0), 0, 0]  # placeholder\n   726→    reward_frac = np.mean(trial_rewarded)\n   727→    ax.bar(['Rewarded', 'Omitted'], [reward_frac, 1 - reward_frac])\n   728→    ax.set_ylabel('Fraction of trials')\n   729→    ax.set_title(f'Reward rate: {reward_frac:.2f}')\n   730→\n   731→    ax = axes[4, 1]\n   732→    trial_lengths = [teleports[valid_trials[i]] - trial_starts[valid_trials[i]] for i in range(len(valid_trials))]\n   733→    ax.hist(trial_lengths, bins=20)\n   734→    ax.set_xlabel('Trial length (frames)')\n   735→    ax.set_ylabel('Count')\n   736→    ax.set_title('Trial length distribution')\n   737→\n   738→    # Panel 7: Inputs overview\n   739→    ax = axes[5, 0]\n   740→    envs = [input_trials[i][1, 0] for trial')\n   745→\n   746→    ax = axes[5, 1]\n   747→    prev_outcomes = [input_trials[i][3, 0] for i in range(len(input_trials))]\n   748→    ax.plot(prev_outcomes, 'o', markersize=2)\n   749→    ax.set_ylabel('Prev outcome')\n   750→    ax.set_xlabel('Trial')\n   751→    ax.set_title('Previous trial outcome')\n   752→\n   753→    plt.tight_layout()\n   754→    plt.savefig(f'processing_{subject_id}_ses{session_id}.png', dpi=100)\n   755→    plt.close()\n   756→    print(f\"  Saved processing plot: processing_{subject_id}_ses{session_id}.png\")\n   757→\n   758→\n   759→def build_dataset(nwb_files, show_processing=False):\n   760→    \"\"\"\n   761→    Build the full dataset from NWB files.\n   762→\n   763→    Returns:\n   764→        data: dict in the target format\n   765→    \"\"\"\n   766→    all_neural = []\n   767→    all_input = []\n   768→    all_output = []\n   769→    all_subject_ids = []\n   770→    all_session_infos = []\n   771→ Hz\nFRAME_PERIOD = 1.0 / IMAGING_RATE  # seconds\nTRACK_LENGTH = 450.0  # cm\nSPEED_THRESHOLD = 2.0  # cm/s\nINTERNEURON_CORR_THRESHOLD = 0.5\nLICK_ERROR_FRACTION = 0.35  # fraction of samples with cumulative lick > 2\nNEUROPIL_COEF = 0.7\nBASELINE_WINDOW = 300  # frames (~20s at 15.5 Hz)\nDFF_SMOOTH_SIGMA = 2  # frames\n\n# Reward zone definitions (cm)\nREWARD_ZONES = {\n    'A': (80, 130),\n    'B': (200, 250),\n    'C': (320, 370),\n}\n\n# Speed bins for output\nSPEED_BINS = [0, 2, 10, 20, 40, np.inf]  # edges: <2, 2-10, 10-20, 20-40, >40\n\n# Position bins (5 equal bins of 90 cm each)\nPOS_BIN_EDGES = np.linspace(0, TRACK_LENGTH, 6)  # [0, 90, 180, 270, 360, 450]\n\n# Distance to reward zone bins\nDIST_BINS = [-np.inf, -50, -10, 0, 0, 10, 50, np.inf]\n# Special handling: bin 3 = exactly 0 (in reward zone)\n\n\n# ============================================================\n# Helper Functions\n# ============================================================\n\ndef get_all_nwb_files(data_dir, sample=False):\n    \"\"\"Get all NWB files organized by subject.\"\"\"\n    subjects = sorted([d for d in os.listdir(data_dir)\n                       if d.startswith('sub-') and os.path.isdir(os.path.join(data_dir, d))])\n\n    all_files = []\n    for subj in subjects:\n        subj_dir = os.path.join(data_dir, subj)\n        nwb_files = sorted([f for f in os.listdir(subj_dir) if f.endswith('.nwb')])\n        for nwb_file in nwb_files:\n            all_files.append({\n                'subject': subj.replace('sub-', ''),\n                'filepath': os.path.join(subj_dir, nwb_file),\n                'filename': nwb_file,\n            })\n\n    if sample:\n        # Select 2 sessions from different subjects for testing\n        # Pick one small (m11) and one larger subject\n        sample_files = []\n        for f in all_files:\n            if f['subject'] == 'm11' and 'ses-03' in f['filename']:\n                sample_files.append(f)\n            elif f['subject'] == 'm3' and 'ses-03' in f['filename']:\n                sample_files.append(f)\n        return sample_files\n\n    return all_files\n\n\ndef compute_dff(F, Fneu, trial_starts, teleports):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute dF/F following the reference code preprocessing.dff().\n\n    Steps:\n    1. Neuropil subtraction (coef=0.7)\n    2. Mask out-of-trial data\n    3. Maximin baseline per trial\n    4. dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\n    5. Gaussian smooth (sigma=2 frames)\n\n    Parameters:\n        F: (n_samples, n_neurons) raw fluorescence\n        Fneu: (n_samples, n_neurons) neuropil fluorescence\n        trial_starts: array of trial start indices\n        teleports: array of trial end indices\n\n    Returns:\n        dff: (n_samples, n_neurons) delta F/F, NaN outside trials\n    \"\"\"\n    n_samples, n_neurons = F.shape\n\n    # Neuropil subtraction\n    F_corr = F - NEUROPIL_COEF * Fneu\n\n    # Initialize dFF with NaN\n    dff = np.full_like(F_corr, np.nan)\n\n    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(trial_starts, teleports)):\n        if start >= n_samples or stop >= n_samples:\n            continue\n        trial_F = F_corr[start:stop, :].copy()\n\n        if len(trial_F) < 10:\n            continue\n\n        # Maximin baseline per trial\n        smoothed = gaussian_filter1d(trial_F, sigma=15, axis=0)\n        min_filtered = minimum_filter1d(smoothed, size=BASELINE_WINDOW, axis=0)\n        baseline = maximum_filter1d(min_filtered, size=BASELINE_WINDOW, axis=0)\n\n        # dF/F\n        abs_baseline = np.abs(baseline)\n        abs_baseline[abs_baseline < 1e-10] = 1e-10\n        trial_dff = (trial_F - baseline) / abs_baseline\n\n        # Gaussian smooth within trial (combined loop)\n        dff[start:stop, :] = gaussian_filter1d(trial_dff, sigma=DFF_SMOOTH_SIGMA, axis=0)\n\n    return dff\n\n\ndef identify_interneurons(dff, speed, iscell_mask):\n    \"\"\"\n    Identify putative interneurons by correlation of dF/F with speed.\n    Vectorized implementation for performance.\n\n    Parameters:\n        dff: (n_samples, n_neurons) delta F/F\n        speed: (n_samples,) speed timeseries\n        iscell_mask: boolean mask for which ROIs are cells\n\n    Returns:\n        is_interneuron: boolean array of length n_iscell (True = interneuron)\n    \"\"\"\n    n_neurons = iscell_mask.sum()\n    dff_cells = dff[:, iscell_mask]  # (n_samples, n_neurons)\n\n    # Find globally valid timepoints (speed is valid)\n    speed_valid = ~np.isnan(speed)\n\n    # For each neuron, valid = non-NaN dff AND non-NaN speed\n    # Since dFF NaN pattern is the same for all neurons (based on trial boundaries),\n    # we can use the first neuron's pattern\n    neuron_valid = ~np.isnan(dff_cells[:, 0]) if n_neurons > 0 else np.zeros(len(speed), dtype=bool)\n    valid = speed_valid & neuron_valid\n\n    if valid.sum() < 100:\n        return np.zeros(n_neurons, dtype=bool)\n\n    # Vectorized Pearson correlation\n    X = dff_cells[valid, :]  # (n_valid, n_neurons)\n    Y = speed[valid]  # (n_valid,)\n\n    # Demean\n    X_mean = X.mean(axis=0, keepdims=True)\n    Y_mean = Y.mean()\n    X_centered = X - X_mean\n    Y_centered = Y - Y_mean\n\n    # Correlation = cov(X, Y) / (std(X) * std(Y))\n    n = valid.sum()\n    cov_XY = (X_centered * Y_centered[:, np.newaxis]).sum(axis=0) / (n - 1)\n    std_X = np.sqrt((X_centered ** 2).sum(axis=0) / (n - 1))\n    std_Y = np.sqrt((Y_centered ** 2).sum() / (n - 1))\n\n    # Avoid division by zero\n    std_X[std_X < 1e-10] = 1e-10\n\n    r = cov_XY / (std_X * std_Y)\n\n    is_interneuron = r > INTERNEURON_CORR_THRESHOLD\n    return is_interneuron\n\n\ndef identify_reward_zone(position, reward_zone_signal, trial_start, trial_end):\n    \"\"\"\n    Identify which reward zone (A, B, or C) is active for a trial.\n\n    Uses position where reward_zone > 0 to determine zone location.\n    \"\"\"\n    pos_trial = position[trial_start:trial_end]\n    rz_trial = reward_zone_signal[trial_start:trial_end]\n\n    in_rz = rz_trial > 0\n    if not np.any(in_rz):\n        return None  # No reward zone entry (omission or no entry)\n\n    rz_pos = pos_trial[in_rz]\n    mean_rz_pos = np.mean(rz_pos)\n\n    # Map to zone A, B, or C based on center position\n    best_zone = None\n    best_dist = np.inf\n    for zone_name, (zone_start, zone_end) in REWARD_ZONES.items():\n        zone_center = (zone_start + zone_end) / 2\n        dist = abs(mean_rz_pos - zone_center)\n        if dist < best_dist:\n            best_dist = dist\n            best_zone = zone_name\n\n    return best_zone\n\n\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position, rz_start, rz_end):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute signed distance from position to nearest point in reward zone.\n\n    Negative = before zone (position < rz_start)\n    Zero = within zone\n    Positive = past zone (position > rz_end)\n    \"\"\"\n    distance = np.zeros_like(position)\n\n    before = position < rz_start\n    inside = (position >= rz_start) & (position <= rz_end)\n    after = position > rz_end\n\n    distance[before] = position[before] - rz_start  # negative\n    distance[inside] = 0.0\n    distance[after] = position[after] - rz_end  # positive\n\n    return distance\n\n\ndef discretize_distance(distance):\n    \"\"\"\n    Discretize distance to reward zone into 7 bins:\n    0: < -50 cm\n    1: -50 to -10 cm\n    2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n    3: 0 cm (in reward zone)\n    4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n    5: +10 to +50 cm\n    6: > +50 cm\n    \"\"\"\n    bins = np.zeros(len(distance), dtype=np.int64)\n\n    bins[distance < -50] = 0\n    bins[(distance >= -50) & (distance < -10)] = 1\n    bins[(distance >= -10) & (distance < 0)] = 2\n    bins[distance == 0] = 3  # in reward zone\n    bins[(distance > 0) & (distance <= 10)] = 4\n    bins[(distance > 10) & (distance <= 50)] = 5\n    bins[distance > 50] = 6\n\n    return bins\n\n\ndef discretize_speed(speed):\n    \"\"\"\n    Discretize speed into 5 bins:\n    0: < 2 cm/s\n    1: 2-10 cm/s\n    2: 10-20 cm/s\n    3: 20-40 cm/s\n    4: > 40 cm/s\n    \"\"\"\n    bins = np.zeros(len(speed), dtype=np.int64)\n\n    bins[speed < 2] = 0\n    bins[(speed >= 2) & (speed < 10)] = 1\n    bins[(speed >= 10) & (speed < 20)] = 2\n    bins[(speed >= 20) & (speed < 40)] = 3\n    bins[speed >= 40] = 4\n\n    return bins\n\n\ndef discretize_position(position):\n    \"\"\"\n    Discretize position into 5 equal bins of 90 cm each.\n    0: 0-90 cm\n    1: 90-180 cm\n    2: 180-270 cm\n    3: 270-360 cm\n    4: 360-450 cm\n    \"\"\"\n    bins = np.clip(np.floor(position / 90.0).astype(np.int64), 0, 4)\n    return bins\n\n\ndef process_session(filepath, show_processing=False, session_idx=0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Process a single NWB file and return trial-organized data.\n\n    Returns:\n        session_data: dict with neural, input, output data per trial\n        session_info: dict with metadata about the session\n    \"\"\"\n    t0 = time.time()\n\n    with h5py.File(filepath, 'r') as f:\n        # ---- Load metadata ----\n        subject_id = f['general']['subject']['subject_id'][()].decode() if isinstance(\n            f['general']['subject']['subject_id'][()], bytes) else str(f['general']['subject']['subject_id'][()])\n        session_id = f['general']['session_id'][()].decode() if isinstance(\n            f['general']['session_id'][()], bytes) else str(f['general']['session_id'][()])\n        imaging_rate = f['general']['optophysiology']['ImagingPlane']['imaging_rate'][()]\n\n        # ---- Load behavioral data ----\n        behav = f['processing']['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries']\n        position = behav['position']['data'][:]\n        speed = behav['speed']['data'][:]\n        lick = behav['lick']['data'][:]\n        reward_zone_signal = behav['reward_zone']['data'][:]\n        trial_start_signal = behav['trial_start']['data'][:]\n        teleport_signal = behav['teleport']['data'][:]\n        trial_number = behav['trial number']['data'][:]\n        environment = behav['environment']['data'][:]\n        scanning = behav['scanning']['data'][:]\n\n        # Reward events\n        reward_data = behav['Reward']['data'][:]\n        reward_timestamps = behav['Reward']['timestamps'][:]\n        behav_timestamps = behav['position']['timestamps'][:]\n\n        # ---- Load neural data ----\n        ophys = f['processing']['ophys']\n        seg = ophys['ImageSegmentation']['PlaneSegmentation']\n        iscell = seg['iscell'][:, 0].astype(bool)\n        plane_idx = seg['planeIdx'][:]\n\n        # Load fluorescence and deconvolved for all planes\n        planes = sorted(ophys['Deconvolved'].keys())\n\n        if len(planes) == 1:\n            deconv_data = ophys['Deconvolved']['plane0']['data'][:]  # (n_samples, n_rois)\n            fluor_data = ophys['Fluorescence']['plane0']['data'][:]\n            neuropil_data = ophys['Neuropil']['plane0']['data'][:]\n            n_planes = 1\n        else:\n            # Multi-plane: concatenate ROIs across planes\n            deconv_parts = []\n            fluor_parts = []\n            neuro_parts = []\n            for plane in planes:\n                deconv_parts.append(ophys['Deconvolved'][plane]['data'][:])\n                fluor_parts.append(ophys['Fluorescence'][plane]['data'][:])\n                neuro_parts.append(ophys['Neuropil'][plane]['data'][:])\n            deconv_data = np.concatenate(deconv_parts, axis=1)\n            fluor_data = np.concatenate(fluor_parts, axis=1)\n            neuropil_data = np.concatenate(neuro_parts, axis=1)\n            n_planes = len(planes)\n\n    t_load = time.time() - t0\n\n    # Handle potential length mismatch between behavioral and neural data\n    n_behav_samples = len(position)\n    n_neural_samples = deconv_data.shape[0]\n    n_samples = min(n_behav_samples, n_neural_samples)\n    if n_behav_samples != n_neural_samples:\n        # Truncate to common length\n        position = position[:n_samples]\n        speed = speed[:n_samples]\n        lick = lick[:n_samples]\n        reward_zone_signal = reward_zone_signal[:n_samples]\n        trial_start_signal = trial_start_signal[:n_samples]\n        teleport_signal = teleport_signal[:n_samples]\n        trial_number = trial_number[:n_samples]\n        environment = environment[:n_samples]\n        scanning = scanning[:n_samples]\n        behav_timestamps = behav_timestamps[:n_samples]\n        deconv_data = deconv_data[:n_samples, :]\n        fluor_data = fluor_data[:n_samples, :]\n        neuropil_data = neuropil_data[:n_samples, :]\n\n    n_total_rois = deconv_data.shape[1]\n\n    # ---- Find trial boundaries ----\n    trial_starts = np.where(trial_start_signal > 0)[0]\n    teleports = np.where(teleport_signal > 0)[0]\n\n    # Ensure matching number of starts and teleports\n    n_trials = min(len(trial_starts), len(teleports))\n    trial_starts = trial_starts[:n_trials]\n    teleports = teleports[:n_trials]\n\n    # Ensure each teleport comes after its corresponding trial start\n    valid = teleports > trial_starts\n    trial_starts = trial_starts[valid]\n    teleports = teleports[valid]\n    n_trials = len(trial_starts)\n\n    # Create final neuron mask: iscell AND not interneuron\n    iscell_indices = np.where(iscell)[0]\n    non_interneuron = ~is_interneuron\n    final_neuron_mask = np.zeros(n_total_rois, dtype=bool)\n    final_neuron_mask[iscell_indices[non_interneuron]] = True\n\n    n_neurons = final_neuron_mask.sum()\n    n_interneurons = is_interneuron.sum()\n\n    # ---- Get deconvolved data for selected neurons ----\n    neural_all = deconv_data[:, final_neuron_mask]  # (n_samples, n_neurons)\n\n    # ---- Process reward events ----\n    # Map reward timestamps to frame indices\n    reward_frame_indices = np.searchsorted(behav_timestamps, reward_timestamps)\n    reward_frame_indices = np.clip(reward_frame_indices, 0, n_samples - 1)\n\n    # Determine which trials have rewards\n    trial_rewarded = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=bool)\n    for t in range(n_trials):\n        start = trial_starts[t]\n        end = teleports[t]\n        trial_rewards = np.any((reward_frame_indices >= start) & (reward_frame_indices < end))\n        trial_rewarded[t] = trial_rewards\n\n    # ---- Identify reward zones per trial ----\n    trial_rz_label = []  # 'A', 'B', 'C', or None\n    trial_rz_start = np.zeros(n_trials)\n    trial_rz_end = np.zeros(n_trials)\n\n    # First pass: identify zones where animal entered\n    last_known_zone = None\n    for t in range(n_trials):\n        zone = identify_reward_zone(position, reward_zone_signal, trial_starts[t], teleports[t])\n        if zone is not None:\n            last_known_zone = zone\n        trial_rz_label.append(zone if zone is not None else last_known_zone)\n\n    # Fill backward for any leading None values\n    if trial_rz_label[0] is None:\n        for t in range(n_trials):\n            if trial_rz_label[t] is not None:\n                for tt in range(t):\n                    trial_rz_label[tt] = trial_rz_label[t]\n                break\n\n    # Set reward zone coordinates\n    for t in range(n_trials):\n        if trial_rz_label[t] is not None:\n            trial_rz_start[t], trial_rz_end[t] = REWARD_ZONES[trial_rz_label[t]]\n        else:\n            # Fallback - shouldn't happen\n            trial_rz_start[t], trial_rz_end[t] = 200, 250\n\n    # Map reward zone labels to indices: A=0, B=1, C=2\n    rz_label_to_idx = {'A': 0, 'B': 1, 'C': 2}\n    trial_rz_idx = np.array([rz_label_to_idx.get(lbl, 0) for lbl in trial_rz_label])\n\n    # ---- Process lick data ----\n    # Following reference: binarize licks (clip to 0/1), apply error correction\n    lick_binary = np.clip(lick, 0, 1).astype(np.float64)\n\n    # Lick error correction per trial\n    lick_error_trials = []\n    for t in range(n_trials):\n        start = trial_starts[t]\n        end = teleports[t]\n        trial_lick = lick[start:end]\n        if len(trial_lick) > 0:\n            frac_bad = np.sum(trial_lick > 2) / len(trial_lick)\n            if frac_bad > LICK_ERROR_FRACTION:\n                lick_binary[start:end] = np.nan\n                lick_error_trials.append(t)\n\n    # Input construction\n        # [0] Time from trial start in seconds (time-varying)\n        time_from_start = np.arange(n_timepoints) * FRAME_PERIOD\n\n        # [1] Environment type (per trial, broadcast)\n        env_val = float(trial_env[t])\n\n        # [2] Trial number (per trial)\n        trial_num = float(t)\n\n        # [3] Previous trial outcome (per trial)\n        prev_outcome = float(prev_trial_outcome[t])\n\n        # Input array: (4,) for per-trial values, (4, n_timepoints) for time-varying\n        # Time from start is time-varying; others are per-trial\n        # Following the spec: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input,)\n        # Since time_from_start is time-varying, we need mixed format\n        # Use (4,) for per-trial inputs and time_from_start as time-varying\n        # Actually, the spec says input can be (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n        # Let's make time_from_start time-varying and others per-trial\n        # We'll pack as: row 0 = time_from_start (time-varying)\n        # rows 1-3 = per-trial values broadcast to time-varying\n        trial_input = np.zeros((4, n_timepoints), dtype=np.float32)\n        trial_input[0, :] = time_from_start\n        trial_input[1, :] = env_val\n        trial_input[2, :] = trial_num\n        trial_input[3, :] = prev_outcome\n\n        # [1] Absolute position (time-varying, discretized)\n        pos_bins = discretize_position(trial_pos)\n\n        # [2] Speed (time-varying, discretized)\n        speed_bins = discretize_speed(trial_speed)\n\n        # [3] Lick (time-varying, binary)\n        lick_vals = np.where(np.isnan(trial_lick), 0, trial_lick).astype(np.int64)\n\n        # [4] Reward zone location (per trial)\n        rz_loc = trial_rz_idx[t]\n\n        # [5] Reward outcome (per trial)\n        reward_out = int(trial_rewarded[t])\n\n        # Output: time-varying outputs as (n_output, n_timepoints), per-trial as (n_output,)\n        # Mix: first 4 are time-varying, last 2 are per-trial\n        # Pack all as (6, n_timepoints) with per-trial broadcast\n        trial_output = np.zeros((6, n_timepoints), dtype=np.int64)\n        trial_output[0, :] = dist_bins\n        trial_output[1, :] = pos_bins\n        trial_output[2, :] = speed_bins\n        trial_output[3, :] = lick_vals\n        trial_output[4, :] = rz_loc  # broadcast per-trial\n        trial_output[5, :] = reward_out  # broadcast per-trial\n\n        neural_trials.append(trial_neural.astype(np.float32))\n        input_trials.append(trial_input)\n        output_trials.append(trial_output)\n        valid_trials.append(t)\n\n    t_total = time.time() - t0\n\n    session_info = {\n        'subject': subject_id,\n        'session': session_id,\n        'n_neurons': n_neurons,\n        'n_interneurons': n_interneurons,\n        'n_iscell': int(iscell.sum()),\n        'n_trials': len(valid_trials),\n        'n_trials_total': n_trials,\n        'n_lick_error_trials': len(lick_error_trials),\n        'n_rewarded': int(trial_rewarded.sum()),\n        'imaging_rate': imaging_rate,\n        'n_planes': n_planes,\n        'load_time': t_load,\n        'dff_time': t_dff,\n        'interneuron_time': t_int,\n        'total_time': t_total,\n        'reward_zones': trial_rz_label,\n        'environments': trial_env.tolist(),\n    }\n\n    print(f\"  {subject_id} ses-{session_id}: {n_neurons} neurons ({n_interneurons} interneurons excluded), \"\n          f\"{len(valid_trials)} trials, {int(trial_rewarded.sum())}/{n_trials} rewarded, \"\n          f\"{len(lick_error_trials)} lick-error trials, \"\n          f\"{t_total:.1f}s\")\n\n    # Both approaches end up keeping nearly all trials, though the AI's is at least correctly implemented. Since the reference's filtering is broken anyway, the practical difference is minimal.\n\nMoving on to neural data, the AI matches the reference by using deconvolved field data, though it also loads fluorescence and neuropil for computing dF/F when detecting interneurons. The core neural source is correct, but the AI then diverges significantly in processing—it filters out interneurons based on their correlation with speed and replaces NaN values with zeros, neither of which the reference does.\n\nThere's a tension here: the paper describes interneuron filtering, but the reference code doesn't implement it. The AI is following the paper's description rather than the code's actual behavior. I'd mark this as concerning since the reference conversion code only filters by iscell, and the AI is adding extra filtering steps beyond that.\n\nMoving through the other comparisons, the neural data alignment matches perfectly—both use trial start with no additional processing. The temporal resolution also aligns, keeping the original ~64.5ms rate. For the time source, there's a discrepancy: the reference pulls timestamps from the behavior time series, while the AI computes them from the frame period constant.  This is a minor concern since constant imaging rates should produce nearly identical results, though actual timestamps are more robust. The time computation differs in approach but yields approximately the same outcome. The alignment of time with neural data matches across both implementations, and I'm now checking the environment type source.\n\nContinuing through the environment processing, I see the reference implementation casts to int directly while the AI version takes a median of valid values—both approaches work since the environment stays constant within a trial. The trial numbering matches exactly between implementations, and I'm examining how previous trial outcomes are being sourced and processed.\n\nFor the previous trial outcome, both check the reward behavior time series, with the AI using a trial_rewarded array to determine if the previous trial had a reward, defaulting to 0 for the first trial. Moving into distance to reward zone calculations, there's a notable difference in how zones are assigned—the reference uses Viterbi decoding while the AI uses mean position, which is concerning methodologically even though the distance calculation itself matches correctly. The discretization approach is being evaluated next. inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, inf] with np.digitize - 1\n- AI: explicit conditional binning with distance == 0 for bin 3\n- Decision: MATCH (functionally equivalent, minor boundary differences)\n- Code: CORRECT\n\n**7-d: Distance alignment**\n- Reference: same indices\n- AI: same indices\n- Decision: MATCH\n- Code: CORRECT\n\n**8-a: Absolute position source**\n- Reference: position behavior time series\n- AI: position behavior time series\n- Decision: MATCH\n- Code: CORRECT\n\n**8-b: Absolute position processing**\n- Reference: none beyond extraction\n- AI: none beyond extraction\n- Decision: MATCH\n- Code: CORRECT\n\n**8-c: Absolute position discretization**\n- Reference: [-inf, 50, 150, 250, 350, inf] (100cm bins covering full range)\n- AI: [0, 90, 180, 270, 360, 450] (90cm bins from 0 to 450)\n- Decision: CONCERNING (different bin boundaries, AI misses positions outside 0-450)\n\nThe AI's 90cm bin size is mathematically sound—450cm corridor divided by 5 bins equals 90cm each—but it clips out-of-range positions instead of handling them with infinite boundaries like the reference does. This means positions below 0 or above 450 get forced into the edge bins rather than being properly bounded.\n\nNow moving to lick processing, where the AI applies extra error correction beyond the basic binarization that the reference uses.\n\nFor reward zone location, there's a mismatch—the reference uses a Viterbi algorithm while the AI takes a simpler mean position approach, which is concerning since they could diverge significantly.\n\nThe reward zone processing also differs: the reference relies on Viterbi for consistent labeling while the AI identifies zones per-trial and fills gaps, which might produce similar results but uses fundamentally different logic.\n\nReward outcomes match between both approaches.\n\nFor missing data handling, both implementations address similar edge cases like length mismatches and short trials, though with different specifics—the reference crops while the AI truncates, and they handle missing zones differently.\n\nThe most time-consuming steps appear to be NWB loading and the full conversion process.\n\nLooking at vectorizable loops, the reference has a per-trial loop and Viterbi inner loop, while the AI's per-trial structure is more of a natural necessity given its approach rather than an optimization opportunity.\n\nOn repeated processing, the reference does redundant NWB file loading between survey and conversion steps, whereas the AI processes each session once without that duplication.\n\nFor unnecessary processing, I need to reconsider what the question is really asking—it's about results that get computed but then discarded downstream. The dF/F computation and interneuron filtering the AI does actually feed into the analysis, so they're not truly unnecessary even though the reference doesn't include them. Both the reference and my analysis say N/A for question 13-d, so they align on unnecessary processing. I'm now looking at the AI's trial boundary detection logic to see if there's something I missed about how it identifies positive frames in the teleport signal.\n\nThe code pairs trial starts with teleports, filters for valid cases where teleports occur after starts, and keeps matching pairs. If the teleport signal fires for multiple consecutive frames per trial, this approach would break down, but the AI's statistics (~80 trials per session) match what I'd expect, suggesting either the signal is single-frame or the code happens to work anyway. This feels fragile enough to warrant a CONCERNING rating.\n\nNow I'm ready to compile all the evaluations systematically. Continuing through the validation checklist, most items are matching correctly, though I'm noticing a potential issue with 1-d—the code uses all positive frames from the teleport signal rather than just the rising edges, which might not align with the original decision to use trial boundaries based on rising edges. I'm realizing the key difference: the AI's approach finds all frames where teleport is positive, while the reference code specifically detects rising edges—transitions from non-positive to positive. These would be equivalent only if teleport signals are single-frame pulses, but if they span multiple frames, the AI's method would incorrectly identify all positive frames as trial boundaries rather than just the onset. More critically, using these indices as exclusive slice endpoints means the AI might be including or excluding the wrong frames from each trial. I'm realizing the key distinction here: for decision correctness, the interneuron filtering is concerning because the reference doesn't mention it, but for code correctness, if the AI decided to filter interneurons, then the implementation itself is correct. I should evaluate both dimensions separately. Now let me think through the temporal resolution question for 2-e and then compile my findings. Now I'm clarifying the task definitions: 2-d asks about per-trial alignment to events (trial start), while 2-e asks about temporal resolution and rebinning. Both the AI and reference approaches keep native resolution without rebinning, so they match on this front. I should note that while the AI uses a different method for detecting trial boundaries, the statistical results align, so this distinction doesn't materially affect the outcome. List the contents of this directory to understand what files are available\\n4. **REMINDER**: Do NOT look at any files outside this directory\\n\\n**Done when**: \\n- `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` exists with the proper template structure\\n- You have confirmed Python and key packages work\\n- You have listed directory contents in CONVERSION_NOTES.md\\n\\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 1, verify that `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` exists by running `ls -la CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. If it doesn't exist, STOP and create it now.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\\n\\n**⚠️ CONSTRAINT REMINDER**: Only look at code in the `code` directory. Do NOT look outside this project directory.\\n\\n**Goal**: Understand how the original authors loaded and processed their data.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Look for documentation or README files in the `code` directory\\n- Read the provided reference code bases to see how the reference paper loaded and manipulated the data\\n- Understand which functions are being called to read, curate, process, and manipulate the data\\n- For neural imaging data, does delta F over F need to be computed? \\n- For electrophysiology neural data, do cells need to be filtered based on quality? \\n- Note to file important functions, their inputs, and outputs\\n\\n**Done when**: You have documented key functions and their purposes in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 1\\\".\\n\\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 2, update Step 1 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 2: Dataset Exploration\\n\\n**⚠️ CONSTRAINT REMINDER**: Only look at data in the `data` directory. Do NOT look outside this project directory.\\n\\n**Goal**: Understand the native data structure\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Identify how data is organized (all files, formats, hierarchies)\\n- Determine available variables and their meanings\\n- Check data dimensions and data types\\n- Look for documentation or README files\\n- Note the data organization and structure\\n- Note dataset size parameters such as the **total number** of subjects (mice), sessions, trials, and neurons\\n\\n**Done when**: You have documented the data structure, file formats, size, and available variables in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 2\\\".\\n\\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 3, update Step 2 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 3: Reference Text Reading\\n\\n**Goal**: Extract key information from the paper and methods.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Scan the reference papers and methods.txt for important details about the data\\n- Look for and note to file:\\n  - Any information about dataset size (number of neurons, subjects, sessions, trials)\\n  - Fraction of data with different values, e.g. fraction of rewarded outcomes\\n  - Data processing, curation, and filtering\\n  - Variables important to experiments\\n  - Temporal alignment\\n  - Temporal binning\\n- Read the reference paper to find information about expected decoder accuracy.\\n\\n**Done when**: You have documented expected dataset statistics and processing details in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 3\\\".\\n\\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 4, update Step 3 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 4: Check for Consistency\\n\\n**Goal**: Verify your understanding is consistent across all sources. \\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Compare your understanding of:\\n  - Important functions from exploring the reference code\\n  - Dataset structure from exploring the dataset\\n  - Analysis described in the reference texts\\n- Note and investigate any discrepancies\\n- In particular, look for discrepancies in:\\n  - Experimental and behavioral variables\\n  - Data processing\\n  - Data curation\\n- Iterate until your understanding of the reference code, dataset, and reference texts are consistent\\n\\n**Done when**: You have resolved all discrepancies and documented your final understanding in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 4\\\".\\n\\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 5, update Step 4 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 5: Mapping Planning\\n\\n**Goal**: Create a detailed plan for mapping source data to target format.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Map source data elements to target format components:\\n  - Which variables contain neural activity?\\n  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\\n  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\\n  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\\n  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\\n  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\\n  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `code` directory. \\n- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\\n  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\\n  - Temporal alignment of trials\\n  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\\n  - Brain regions recorded from\\n- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\\n- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\\n- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\\n- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\\n\\n**Done when**:\\n- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \\\"Step 5\\\"\\n- You have documented all decisions under \\\"Step 5\\\"\\n- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \\\"Step 5\\\"\\n\\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 6: Script Development\\n\\n**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `convert_data.py`.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Write all conversion code in the file `convert_data.py`\\n- Script should run as **`python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\\n- Include options:\\n  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\\n  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\\n  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\\n- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\\n- Write efficient code:\\n  - Vectorize loops\\n  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\\n  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\\n  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\\n- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\\n  - There are no temporal misalignments\\n  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\\n- Write clear, well-documented functions\\n- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\\n- Validate data shapes and types at each step\\n- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\\n\\n**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\\n\\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\\n\\n**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n1. Run `python -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee conversion_sample_out.txt`\\n  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\\n  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\\n  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \\n  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\\n2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\\n  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \\n  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \\n  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \\n3. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_sample_out.txt`.\\n4. Analyze the output file `verification_sample_out.txt`:\\n  a. Verify no errors reported.\\n  b. Attempt to address any warnings\\n  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\\n\\n**Done when**: \\n- `sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\\n- `verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\\n- Processing plots show no anomalies\\n- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 7\\\"\\n\\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `sample_data.pkl` and `verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\\n\\n**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\\n2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\\n3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\\n\\n**Investigate any issues**:\\n- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\\n\\n**Done when**: \\n- Decoder training completes without errors\\n- Loss decreases over epochs\\n- Accuracies are above chance\\n- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 8\\\"\\n\\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\\n\\n**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\\n2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\\n3. Run `python -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee conversion_full_out.txt`\\n4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \\n5. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \\n6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \\n7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\\n8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\\n9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\\n\\n**Done when**:\\n- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\\n- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\\n- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\\n- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 9\\\"\\n\\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 10: Critical Review 1\\n\\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\\n\\n**Check 1: Output log verification**\\nRead through `verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \\n\\n**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\\n- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\\n- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\\n- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \\n- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \\n- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \\n- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \\n\\n**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\\n- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \\n- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \\n- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \\n- Fix any mismatches. \\n- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \\n- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\\n\\n**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\\n- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference paper. \\n- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \\n- Fix any discovered issues. \\n- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \\n\\n**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\\n- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \\n- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \\n\\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\\n\\nWrite a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 10\\\".\\n\\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\\n\\n**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\\n\\n**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \\n2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\\n3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\\n4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the paper. \\n\\n**Done when**: \\n1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\\n2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 11\\\" with a table of accuracies\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 12: Critical Review 2\\n\\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\\n\\n**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\\n- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\\n- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \\n- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \\n\\n**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to paper**\\n- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference paper\\n- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the paper's accuracy for each variable\\n- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \\n- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \\n\\n**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\\n- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \\n\\n**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\\n1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\\n2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\\n3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\\n4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \\n5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and paper. \\n\\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\\n\\n**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \\n\\n**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 12\\\".\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\\n\\n**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\\n  - Key decisions and rationale\\n  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\\n  - Validation results and tables\\n  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\\n- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\\n  - Dataset description\\n  - How to load and use the converted data\\n  - Output format specification\\n  - Key statistics\\n- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\\n- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\\n\\n**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\\n\\n**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. Discretization bins:\\n  - 0:  < -50 cm\\n  - 1: -50 to -10 cm\\n  - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\\n  - 3: 0 cm\\n  - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\\n  - 5: +10 to +50 cm\\n  - 6: > +50 cm\\n- Absolute position in corridor, discretized into 5 equal-sized bins, time-varying\\n- Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:\\n  - 0: < 2 cm/s\\n  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\\n  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\\n  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\\n  - 4: > 40 cm/s\\n- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\\n- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\\n- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\\n\\n## Target Data Format\\n\\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\\n\\n```python\\ndata = {\\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\\n\\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\\n    ] # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\\n\\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'metadata': {\\n        'task_description': str, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\\n        ...\\n    }\\n}\\n```\\n\\n### Key Format Requirements\\n\\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \\n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\\n\\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \\n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \\n\\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \\n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \\n\\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\\n\\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\\n\\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \\n\\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\\n\\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \\n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \\n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\\n\\n## Python environment\\n\\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\\n\\n## Decoder Reference\\n\\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\\n- Optional arguments:\\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\\n\\n### Purpose\\n\\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\\n1. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \\n2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\\n3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\\n4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the paper. \\n\\n**Done when**: \\n1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\\n2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 11\\\" with a table of accuracies\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 12: Critical Review 2\\n\\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\\n\\n**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\\n- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\\n- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \\n- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \\n\\n**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to paper**\\n- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference paper\\n- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the paper's accuracy for each variable\\n- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \\n- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \\n\\n**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\\n- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \\n\\n**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\\n1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\\n2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\\n3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\\n4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \\n5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and paper. \\n\\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\\n\\n**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \\n\\n**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \\\"Step 12\\\".\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\\n\\n**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\\n\\n**Actions**:\\n- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\\n  - Key decisions and rationale\\n  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\\n  - Validation results and tables\\n  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\\n- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\\n  - Dataset description\\n  - How to load and use the converted data\\n  - Output format specification\\n  - Key statistics\\n- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\\n- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\\n\\n**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\\n\\n**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. How are **all the data** for all subjects, sessions, and trials loaded in?\n\ni. The AI finds all subdirectories of `data/` starting with `sub-`, collects all `.nwb` files within each, and loads them using `h5py` (not `pynwb`). Finding all NWB files:\n```python\ndef get_all_nwb_files(data_dir, sample=False):\n    subjects = sorted([d for d in os.listdir(data_dir)\n                       if d.startswith('sub-') and os.path.isdir(os.path.join(data_dir, d))])\n    all_files = []\n    for subj in subjects:\n        subj_dir = os.path.join(data_dir, subj)\n        nwb_files = sorted([f for f in os.listdir(subj_dir) if f.endswith('.nwb')])\n        for nwb_file in nwb_files:\n            all_files.append({\n                'subject': subj.replace('sub-', ''),\n                'filepath': os.path.join(subj_dir, nwb_file),\n                'filename': nwb_file,\n            })\n    return all_files\n```\n\nLoading data from NWB:\n```python\nwith h5py.File(filepath, 'r') as f:\n    behav = f['processing']['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries']\n    position = behav['position']['data'][:]\n    ...\n    ophys = f['processing']['ophys']\n    deconv_data = ophys['Deconvolved']['plane0']['data'][:]\n```\n\niii.
